# Everyone is Getting AI Fluency Wrong—Steal My 10 Level Framework That Exposes the Real AI Skill Gap

## Summary
Nate B Jones presents a 10-level AI fluency framework that moves beyond tool-specific skills to assess fundamental capabilities. The framework reveals that 80% of users plateau at levels 1-5 (basic usage to mental models), while levels 5-7 require systems thinking, and levels 7-9 demand teaching and innovation capabilities. The strategic insight: AI fluency isn't about mastering specific tools—it's about developing transferable mental models, systematic approaches, and the ability to discover undocumented capabilities. This creates a durable competitive advantage as the technology evolves.

---

## 1. Context

**Background:** Organizations struggle to assess AI competency because existing frameworks focus on tool-specific skills (ChatGPT prompts, Copilot usage) rather than transferable principles. As AI capabilities rapidly evolve, skill assessments become obsolete within months. The speaker addresses a fundamental question: "How do I level up in AI, and how do I know I'm improving?" without creating a framework tied to specific models or tools.

**Why This Matters:** For business leaders, understanding AI fluency levels enables:
- Accurate assessment of team capabilities
- Strategic hiring and development decisions
- Identification of capability gaps before they become competitive disadvantages
- Resource allocation for training based on actual skill requirements
- Recognition that "baseline" competency is shifting rapidly upward

**Key Stats:**
- 80% of users operate at levels 1-5 (basic usage through mental models)
- Most people end up below level 5 on this "tough scale"
- The competitive baseline is shifting: "the whole population is going to grow into one to three in the next year"
- Timeline warning: "It's never going to go slower than it's going right now" (regarding AI adoption pace)
- Framework spans October 2025 into 2026, acknowledging rapid evolution

---

## 2. Vision & Why

**Core Mission:** Create an evergreen, model-agnostic framework for assessing and developing AI fluency that remains relevant as specific tools and models evolve. The mission is to provide "a living breathing framework that helps you make sense of your own skill level relative to where we are with AI that you can come back to again and again."

**The "Why" Behind It:**
1. **Lack of Comprehensive Assessment:** "There hasn't really been a comprehensive approach that is agnostic of models, that doesn't care if you're a Chad GPT user or a Copilot user or a Claude user. It just focuses on the principles and your level of understanding."

2. **Wrong Teaching Approach:** "I have a strong conviction that AI is a generalist skill set and we are probably teaching it wrong if we dive too deep into verticals without that generalist conceptual foundation."

3. **Missing Foundation Layer:** "We really haven't had that. And that's what I'm setting out to do here. This sort of general approach to skill sets and fluency."

4. **Career Development Clarity:** Enables individuals to "understand your level and what your goals are and make sure you are equipped to get there."

**Enduring Nature:**
- **Timeless Principles:**
  - Mental models of how LLMs work (next token prediction, context retrieval)
  - Systems thinking and systematization
  - Teaching as a learning multiplier
  - Discovery-oriented innovation mindset
  
- **Time-Specific Elements (2024-2026):**
  - Specific tools mentioned (ChatGPT, Claude, Copilot)
  - Agent frameworks launched October 6th, 2025
  - Context window sizes (book-sized prompts)
  - Current capability boundaries

---

## 3. Strategic Engine

**How This Actually Works:**

The framework operates as a progressive capability ladder where each level builds on previous foundations while adding distinct competencies. Unlike traditional training (tool → application), this framework works backwards from outcomes:

1. **Assessment Phase:** User identifies current level based on behavioral indicators and mental model sophistication
2. **Gap Analysis:** User identifies target level based on role requirements and career goals
3. **Development Planning:** Custom 90-day plans address specific capability gaps
4. **Iterative Refinement:** Framework adapts as new AI capabilities emerge

**Key Components:**

1. **Levels 1-3: Basic to Intuitive Usage**
   - Tool familiarity (rewriting emails, adjusting documents)
   - Beginning to understand prompting patterns
   - Moving from random trial to intuitive prompt engineering

2. **Levels 3-5: Mental Model Development**
   - Understanding how LLMs actually work (next token prediction, no true "knowing")
   - Grasping context retrieval mechanisms
   - Thinking backwards from desired outputs
   - "You're going to stop asking what should I tell the AI and at this stage you're going to start asking what is the output that I need?"

3. **Levels 5-7: Systems Thinking**
   - Building auditable, repeatable patterns
   - Optimizing for "prompt yield" (quality output per unit of prompting)
   - Creating team-scalable systems
   - Having 5-7 AI tools with specific use cases
   - Maintaining prompt libraries

4. **Levels 7-9: Teaching and Innovation**
   - Documenting learning for others
   - Leading organizational AI adoption
   - Discovering undocumented AI capabilities
   - "Pull the impossible problems into the realm of the possible"

5. **Level 10: Intentionally Withheld**
   - "I don't give tens" - keeps aspiration open-ended

**Why This Works:**

1. **Model Agnostic:** Survives tool churn by focusing on transferable principles
2. **Self-Assessment Friendly:** Behavioral indicators enable honest self-evaluation
3. **Goal-Oriented:** "Not everyone has to be a 10, right? Like that's not the point."
4. **Compound Learning:** Each level creates foundation for exponential growth at next level
5. **Realistic Benchmarking:** "This is a tough scale" - sets appropriate expectations

---

## 4. Behavioral Design

**Behavioral Principles:**

1. **Outcome-First Thinking (Levels 3-5):**
   - Shift from "what should I tell the AI?" to "what output do I need?"
   - Mental models enable working backwards from desired results
   - "I can work back in my head" based on understanding how outputs are generated

2. **Systematic Documentation (Levels 5-7):**
   - Moving from "usually do this" to "this is the sequence I follow"
   - Creating predictable, auditable patterns
   - Building prompt libraries and tool-task associations

3. **Teaching as Learning (Levels 7-9):**
   - "Teaching has been super helpful in driving clarity and revealing gaps in my own understanding"
   - Documentation scales influence and clarifies thinking
   - "The more you document about what you're learning and what you're thinking and how you're growing, the more you're able to scale your influence"

4. **Discovery Orientation (Levels 7-9):**
   - Understanding that "AI capabilities are not all documented"
   - "We do not ship an AI and OpenAI knows all about it. We ship an AI and we all collectively discover the capabilities it has"
   - Treating AI systems as "grown" rather than "programmed"

**Incentive Structure:**

**Encourages:**
- Continuous learning through teaching
- Systems thinking over one-off solutions
- Mental model development over rote memorization
- Efficiency through prompt yield optimization
- Public documentation and knowledge sharing

**Discourages:**
- Tool-specific skill hoarding
- Inefficient trial-and-error prompting
- Staying in comfort zone levels
- Vertical specialization without horizontal foundation
- Passive consumption without production

**Alignment Mechanisms:**

1. **Self-Assessment Checkpoints:** Framework provides clear behavioral indicators for each level
2. **90-Day Development Plans:** Structured progression between levels
3. **Comprehensive Assessment Prompt:** Tool for accurate level identification
4. **Public Accountability:** Encouragement to document and share learning
5. **Shifting Baseline Warning:** "Your competitive reality is shifting" creates urgency

---

## 5. Time & Attention

**Where Time Flows:**

**Levels 1-3 (Basic Users - 80% of population):**
- Time spent: Learning specific tool interfaces, trial-and-error prompting
- Attention on: Immediate task completion
- Efficiency: 10 iterations to get one usable output

**Levels 3-5 (Mental Model Builders):**
- Time spent: Understanding underlying mechanisms, building intuition
- Attention on: How outputs are generated, context management
- Shift: From "what to say" to "what output needed"

**Levels 5-7 (Systems Thinkers):**
- Time spent: Creating reusable patterns, optimizing workflows
- Attention on: Prompt yield, team scalability, feedback loops
- Efficiency: "One or two prompts and get 98% of the way there and move on"
- Investment: Building prompt libraries, tool evaluation, pattern documentation

**Levels 7-9 (Teachers/Innovators):**
- Time spent: Public documentation, curriculum development, capability discovery
- Attention on: Pulling "impossible problems into the realm of the possible"
- Output: Training programs, public content, novel applications

**What This System DOESN'T Spend On:**

1. **Tool-Specific Training:** "I am not giving you the fluency levels for engineers and then the fluency levels for PMs"
2. **Vertical Specialization:** Avoiding deep-dive vertical skills without horizontal foundation
3. **Exhaustive Coverage:** "I'm not going to do all 10 because people just don't stick around for that"
4. **Perfect Prompting:** "Maybe you do [copy prompts], maybe you don't sometimes"
5. **Building AI Systems:** "None of this means you can build an AI system. None of this means that you can build a context window like a rag system or a memory system."

**Allocation Philosophy:**

"Value the tokens, value the time that we are taking with AI so that we can go on to other things. And it is much much much more efficient to just do the prompt correctly and just get the right answer."

Core principle: **Optimize for prompt yield** - maximize quality output per unit of effort invested in prompting. This creates time for higher-value activities (systems building, teaching, innovation) rather than grinding through inefficient iterations.

---

## 6. Moats & Time Horizon

**Competitive Advantages:**

1. **Mental Model Moat (Levels 3-5):**
   - Deep understanding of how LLMs work creates intuitive prompting ability
   - Transferable across all AI tools as they evolve
   - Difficult to teach/replicate because it requires conceptual shift
   - "You have the beginnings of a mental model of what AI can do"

2. **Systems Thinking Moat (Levels 5-7):**
   - Auditable, repeatable patterns that compound over time
   - Prompt libraries and documented workflows as organizational assets
   - "I get a predictable result. I know how to get the predictable result and I can start to systematize it in a way that others can do it too."
   - Team force multiplication through systematization

3. **Teaching/Innovation Moat (Levels 7-9):**
   - Network effects from teaching: "Teaching drives your own learning"
   - Discovering undocumented capabilities before competitors
   - "More accurate to say these systems are grown than to say that they are programmed. And so we're all discovering together what grew."
   - First-mover advantage on novel applications

4. **Evergreen Framework Moat:**
   - Model-agnostic approach survives tool churn
   - "Not meant to be an October 2025 artifact and we're done. It is actually meant to be a living breathing framework"
   - Ability to map new technical launches onto capability assessment

**Time Horizon:**

**Short-Term Benefits (0-3 months):**
- Accurate self-assessment of current capabilities
- Clear development path via 90-day plans
- Immediate efficiency gains through prompt yield optimization
- Quick wins from mental model application

**Medium-Term Benefits (3-12 months):**
- Team systematization creating force multiplication
- Prompt library as organizational asset
- Recognition as go-to AI resource within organization
- Efficiency gap widens vs. non-systematic users

**Long-Term Compound Effects (12+ months):**
- Teaching creates exponential learning curve
- Discovery of novel capabilities creates competitive edge
- Systems and documentation become durable assets
- Framework adapts to new AI launches without rebuilding from scratch
- "The whole population is going to grow into one to three in the next year" - staying ahead requires continuous progression

**Why Time Is Your Friend:**

1. **Compound Learning:** Each level creates exponential foundation for next
2. **Network Effects:** Teaching multiplies personal learning while scaling impact
3. **Asset Accumulation:** Prompt libraries, systems, documentation accumulate value
4. **Adaptability:** Mental models + systems thinking = rapid adaptation to new tools
5. **Widening Gap:** "Your competitive reality is shifting" - early movers compound advantages as baseline rises

**Critical Warning:** "Think of it as a moving train and it's never going to go slower than it's going right now. So, hop aboard and get yourself going as quick as you can."

---

## 7. Flywheels & Lock-In

**Primary Flywheel: The Teaching-Learning-Systems Loop**

**Flywheel Visualization:**

[Use AI → Build Mental Models] → 
[Create Systems & Document] → 
[Teach Others / Discover Capabilities] → 
[Feedback Reveals Gaps & Novel Use Cases] → 
[Deeper Understanding & Better Systems] → 
[Back to Use AI, now at higher level]

**Detailed Flywheel Mechanics:**

1. **Initial Use (Levels 1-3):** Basic AI usage for task completion
2. **Mental Model Development (Levels 3-5):** Understanding how LLMs work enables intuitive prompting
3. **Systematization (Levels 5-7):** Patterns emerge, get documented, become repeatable
4. **Teaching Requirement (Levels 7-9):** To teach, must clarify thinking and close knowledge gaps
5. **Gap Discovery:** Teaching reveals what you don't fully understand yet
6. **Capability Discovery:** Deep usage discovers undocumented AI capabilities
7. **System Improvement:** New discoveries improve existing systems
8. **Enhanced Usage:** Return to step 1 with significantly better capabilities

**Why This Accelerates:**

- Mental models make each interaction more efficient (prompt yield improves)
- Systems create reusable assets (don't rebuild from scratch)
- Teaching forces clarity (can't teach what you don't understand)
- Discovery creates proprietary knowledge (competitive advantage)
- Better systems attract more complex problems (skill development accelerates)

**Lock-In Mechanisms:**

1. **Sunk Cost in Mental Models:**
   - "You need an overarching perspective on the fluency and competency assessment"
   - Mental models are hard-won and highly valuable
   - Switching costs: rebuilding conceptual understanding from scratch

2. **Asset Accumulation:**
   - Prompt libraries specific to your workflows
   - "Five to seven tools they're working with regularly in the AI space"
   - Documented systems and patterns
   - Cost to abandon: losing organizational memory and efficiency

3. **Network Effects:**
   - "Seen by their teammates as a peer collaborator and peer leader"
   - Teaching relationships create dependencies
   - Organizational systems others now rely on
   - Cost to abandon: breaking team dependencies

4. **Identity Lock-In:**
   - Progression from "AI user" to "AI teacher" to "AI innovator"
   - Public documentation creates reputation
   - "Documentarian" identity: "the more you document... the more you're able to scale your influence"
   - Cost to abandon: loss of positioning and influence

5. **Discovery Investment:**
   - Time spent discovering undocumented capabilities
   - Novel applications and use cases found
   - First-mover advantages on innovations
   - Cost to abandon: proprietary knowledge advantage

**Compounding Effect:**

"Most teachers will tell you regardless of subject that that's true" - referring to teaching driving learning.

The compounding happens at multiple levels simultaneously:
- **Individual:** Each level makes next level more accessible
- **Team:** Systems enable others to operate at higher levels
- **Organization:** Documentation creates institutional knowledge
- **Field:** Public teaching advances collective understanding

**Anti-Flywheel Warning:**

"The competitive baseline is shifting upward" - NOT progressing means falling behind in relative terms even if absolute skill stays constant. The flywheel must keep spinning to maintain position.

---

**Winners:**

1. **Early Adopters at Any Level:**
   - Anyone who starts systematic skill development now
   - "Hop aboard and get yourself going as quick as you can"
   - Benefits: Stay ahead of shifting baseline, compound learning advantages
   - Magnitude: "The whole population is going to grow into one to three in the next year"

2. **Systems Thinkers (Levels 5-7):**
   - People who systematize AI workflows
   - Benefits: Massive efficiency gains through prompt yield optimization
   - Example: "If you're prompting inefficiently, you might take 10 iterations to get one usable output. But if you're prompting efficiently, you might do one or two prompts and get 98% of the way there"
   - Organizational value: Can scale patterns to entire teams

3. **Teachers and Documentarians (Levels 7-9):**
   - Those who publicly share learning
   - Benefits: Learning acceleration through teaching, influence, career advancement
   - "Teaching has been super helpful in driving clarity and revealing gaps in my own understanding"
   - Become recognized experts and organizational leaders

4. **Innovators/Discoverers (Levels 7-9):**
   - Those who discover undocumented AI capabilities
   - Benefits: First-mover advantages, competitive differentiation
   - "We're all discovering together what grew" - but systematic discoverers find more
   - Can "pull the impossible problems into the realm of the possible"

5. **Organizations That Adopt Framework:**

   - Benefits: Accurate capability assessment, efficient training allocation, competitive talent development
   - Can identify and close capability gaps strategically
   - "Setting up the AI training curriculum at work" as a Level 7-9 output

6. **Generalists Over Specialists:**
   - Those who build horizontal foundation before vertical depth
   - "AI is a generalist skill set and we are probably teaching it wrong if we dive too deep into verticals without that generalist conceptual foundation"
   - Benefits: Adaptability across tools, transferable skills, future-proofing

**Losers:**

1. **Tool-Specific Specialists:**
   - Those who invest heavily in mastering specific tools without understanding principles
   - Risk: "Model-agnostic" framework makes tool-specific expertise less valuable
   - When tools change: "This comprehensive approach that is agnostic of models"

2. **Passive Users (Stuck at Levels 1-3):**
   - "Most people end up below five"
   - Risk: Baseline shifts upward, relative capability declines
   - By 2026: Level 1-3 becomes the new "zero"

3. **Those Who Resist Systematization:**
   - People who prefer ad-hoc, intuitive approaches without documentation
   - Miss efficiency gains: 10x difference in prompt yield
   - Can't scale their skills to teams

4. **Non-Teachers:**
   - Those who hoard knowledge rather than share
   - Miss learning acceleration from teaching
   - "Teaching drives your own learning" - they lose this multiplier

5. **Organizations with Old Training Models:**
   - Companies still teaching tool-specific skills
   - Investing in training that becomes obsolete with each model update
   - Missing the "generalist conceptual foundation"

6. **Those Who Wait:**
   - "It's never going to go slower than it's going right now"
   - Opportunity cost compounds: gap widens daily
   - By 2026: "Much larger part of the population growing into that sort of 3 to five area"

**Ethical Considerations:**

1. **Accessibility Concern:**
   - Framework requires time investment and access to AI tools
   - "Your goal may not be to be a teacher or an instructor. Maybe your goal is to be a systems thinker"

2. **Experience Gap Widening:**
   - Creates identifiable "haves" and "have-nots" in AI capability
   - "Will be seen by their teammates as a peer collaborator and peer leader"
   - Risk: Organizational hierarchy based on AI fluency

3. **Pressure to Constantly Upskill:**
   - "Think of it as a moving train"
   - May create unsustainable pressure for continuous learning
   - Not everyone needs to reach Level 10

4. **Mental Models as Gatekeeping:**
   - Understanding "how LLMs work" may exclude some learners
   - Technical/conceptual barrier to progression
   - Counter: Explained in accessible terms

5. **Public Documentation Pressure:**
   - Teaching/documentation as requirement for highest levels
   - May disadvantage introverts or those in confidential work
   - "Maybe your goal is to be a systems thinker. I don't know. Or maybe you're perfectly happy just understanding how LLMs work."

**Tension to Monitor:**
The framework democratizes AI learning (free, accessible) while simultaneously creating clear hierarchy of capability that could stratify workforces. The ethical balance depends on organizations using it for development rather than exclusion.

---

## 9. System Health Metric

**What to Optimize For: Prompt Yield**

**Definition:** "Prompt yield is like what is your quality output per unit of prompting."

**The ONE Metric:** 
**Prompt Yield = (Usable Outputs) / (Prompting Iterations)**

This metric captures:
- Efficiency of AI interaction
- Quality of mental models
- Effectiveness of systematization
- Maturity of understanding

**Why This Metric:**

1. **Spans All Levels:**
   - Level 1-3: Very low prompt yield (10 iterations → 1 output)
   - Level 3-5: Improving yield through mental models
   - Level 5-7: High yield through systems (1-2 prompts → 98% solution)
   - Level 7-9: Compound yield through teaching/discovery

2. **Captures True Value:**
   - Not about activity (number of prompts)
   - Focuses on outcomes (usable outputs)
   - "Value the tokens, value the time that we are taking with AI so that we can go on to other things"

3. **Predictive of Progression:**
   - Low yield = stuck in early levels
   - Improving yield = mental models developing
   - Consistently high yield = systems thinking achieved
   - Teaching others to achieve high yield = mastery

4. **Operationally Relevant:**
   - Directly impacts productivity
   - Scales across teams
   - Measurable and improvable
   - "Much much much more efficient to just do the prompt correctly and just get the right answer"

5. **Future-Proof:**
   - Works regardless of which AI tool
   - Remains relevant as models improve
   - Adapts to new capabilities (agents, etc.)

**Alternative/Secondary Metrics:**

While prompt yield is primary, track these at different levels:

- **Level 1-3:** Task completion time vs. baseline
- **Level 3-5:** Intuitive prompt success rate (first try)
- **Level 5-7:** Number of reusable systems/patterns created
- **Level 7-9:** Number of people taught, novel capabilities discovered

**How to Measure:**

**Individual Level:**
1. **Track Over 1 Week:**
   - Count every AI interaction
   - Mark outputs as "usable" (90%+ ready) or "unusable"
   - Count iterations required to reach usable output
   
2. **Calculate:**
   - Prompt Yield = Usable Outputs / Total Prompting Sessions
   - Target progression: 10% → 30% → 50% → 80%+

3. **Benchmark Examples:**
   - "10 iterations to get one usable output" = 10% yield
   - "One or two prompts and get 98% of the way there" = 50-100% yield

**Team Level:**
1. **System Audit:**
   - Count documented AI systems/patterns in use
   - Measure adoption rate across team
   - Track time-to-competency for new team members using systems

2. **Efficiency Delta:**
   - Compare task completion time: systematic vs. ad-hoc users
   - "Yield gap" between team members indicates training opportunities

**Organizational Level:**
1. **Capability Assessment:**
   - Percentage of team at each fluency level
   - Distribution should shift rightward over time
   - Red flag: Majority stuck below Level 5

2. **Innovation Output:**
   - Novel AI applications discovered
   - "Impossible problems" now solved
   - Indicates presence of Level 7-9 practitioners

**Warning Signals (Poor System Health):**
- Prompt yield declining over time (regression)
- Everyone stuck at same level (no progression)
- High time investment, low output quality
- No systematic documentation emerging
- No one teaching/sharing discoveries

**Health Signals:**
- Prompt yield improving week-over-week
- Team distribution shifting to higher levels
- Prompt libraries growing
- Cross-team knowledge sharing increasing
- Novel use cases emerging

---

## 10. Unique Insights & Quotes

### Memorable Quotes

> "There hasn't really been a comprehensive approach that is agnostic of models, that doesn't care if you're a Chad GPT user or a Copilot user or a Claude user. It just focuses on the principles and your level of understanding and helps you to scale it up."

> "Spoiler alert, most people end up below five. This is a tough scale. Don't be afraid."

> "Not everyone has to be a 10, right? Like that's not the point. The point is to understand your level and what your goals are and make sure you are equipped to get there."

> "You're going to stop asking what should I tell the AI and at this stage you're going to start asking what is the output that I need? Because the mental models are going to inform your understanding of how it creates the outputs and you're naturally going to start to say, 'Okay, I get a sense of how the sausage is made, right?' And so this is the output I want. I can work back in my head."

> "If you're prompting inefficiently, you might take 10 iterations to get one usable output. But if you're prompting efficiently, you might do one or two prompts and get 98% of the way there and move on."

> "Value the tokens, value the time that we are taking with AI so that we can go on to other things. And it is much much much more efficient to just do the prompt correctly and just get the right answer."

> "Teaching has been super helpful in driving clarity and revealing gaps in my own understanding that I have to relentlessly close. Most teachers will tell you regardless of subject that that's true."

> "It's more accurate to say these systems are grown than to say that they are programmed. And so we're all discovering together what grew."

> "Your goal is to pull the impossible problems into the realm of the possible. That's what that innovation piece looks like."

> "Think of it as a moving train and it's never going to go slower than it's going right now. So, hop aboard and get yourself going as quick as you can in a way that you feel comfortable that feels aligned with your goals."

> "I have a strong conviction that AI is a generalist skill set and we are probably teaching it wrong if we dive too deep into verticals without that generalist conceptual foundation."

> "The whole population is going to grow into one to three in the next year and there will be a much larger part of the population growing into that sort of 3 to five area and there will be many more people who are pushing themselves up the skill ladder from there."

### Non-Obvious Insights

- **The Baseline is Shifting, Not Static:** Most fluency discussions treat competency as fixed. The critical insight: "Your competitive reality is shifting" - being static means falling behind in relative terms. What's Level 3 in late 2025 will be Level 1 by end of 2026. Strategic implication: Skill development isn't about reaching a destination; it's about maintaining velocity on a moving treadmill.

- **Mental Models Trump Tool Mastery:** "None of this means you can build an AI system. None of this means that you can build a context window like a rag system or a memory system." The non-obvious wisdom: You don't need technical building skills to achieve high fluency. Understanding *how* systems work matters more than building them yourself. This inverts the typical tech skill hierarchy.

- **Teaching is Discovery, Not Transfer:** "Teaching has been super helpful in driving clarity and revealing gaps in my own understanding that I have to relentlessly close." The counterintuitive insight: Teaching isn't just about transferring known knowledge - it's primarily a discovery mechanism for finding your own gaps. The value accrues more to the teacher than the student in terms of learning acceleration.

- **AI Capabilities Are Emergent, Not Documented:** "We do not ship an AI and OpenAI knows all about it. We ship an AI and we all collectively discover the capabilities it has because it's more accurate to say these systems are grown than to say that they are programmed." This reframes AI from "software to learn" to "organism to discover." Strategic implication: First-movers in discovering capabilities have advantages that can't be replicated by reading documentation.

- **Prompt Yield as North Star Over Raw Usage:** The framework optimizes for quality-per-iteration, not quantity of interactions. Most AI strategies optimize for "hours spent with AI" or "number of prompts." The non-obvious insight: "10 iterations to get one usable output" is far worse than "one or two prompts and get 98% of the way there" even if total time is similar. This inverts typical "practice makes perfect" wisdom - deliberate, systematic practice matters more than volume.

- **Systems Thinking is the Bottleneck, Not Access:** 80% plateau before Level 5 not because they lack access to tools, but because they haven't made the mental leap to systematization. "You're thinking in terms of usually do this and they're going to move that over to this is the sequence I follow. I get a predictable result." The non-obvious barrier: conceptual shift from ad-hoc to systematic is the constraint, not tool availability or training time.

- **Fluency is Generalist, Application is Specialist:** "AI is a generalist skill set and we are probably teaching it wrong if we dive too deep into verticals without that generalist conceptual foundation." This contradicts most corporate training which teaches "AI for marketing" or "AI for developers." The insight: Horizontal fluency first, then vertical application. Trying to build vertical skills without horizontal foundation creates brittle, non-transferable capabilities.

- **The "Tough Scale" Creates Better Signal:** "This is a tough scale. Don't be afraid." By intentionally making the framework difficult (most people below 5, withholding 10s), it creates better differentiation and more honest self-assessment. Counterintuitive: Making assessment easier doesn't help anyone - it obscures true capability gaps. The strategic value is in accurate signal, not feel-good scores.

- **Documentarian is a Competency, Not a Side Activity:** "Try to be if you're at this level a documentarian. And what I mean by that is the more you document about what you're learning and what you're thinking and how you're growing, the more you're able to scale your influence." Documentation isn't auxiliary to mastery - it *is* a core competency at advanced levels. This elevates communication from support skill to primary skill.

- **Framework Longevity Through Abstraction:** The framework survives tool churn by abstracting one level above specific implementations. "Not meant to be an October 2025 artifact and we're done. It is actually meant to be a living breathing framework." The non-obvious design principle: Durability comes from appropriate abstraction level - too specific becomes obsolete, too abstract becomes useless. This framework targets mental models and behaviors that persist across tool generations.

## 11. Application & Mental Model

### When to Use This Pattern

**Use this framework when:**

1. **Assessing AI Capability Gaps:**
   - Team can use AI tools but outcomes are inconsistent
   - Unclear why some people are 10x more effective with same tools
   - Need objective measure for hiring/promotion decisions
   - Signal: Wide variance in AI effectiveness despite similar tool access

2. **Planning Training Investments:**
   - Deciding between tool-specific training vs. foundational training
   - Allocating L&D budget across skill levels
   - Need ROI justification for AI upskilling
   - Signal: "We need AI training" without clarity on what kind

3. **Building Internal AI Capabilities:**
   - Scaling AI adoption beyond early adopters
   - Creating internal AI champions/leaders
   - Establishing AI centers of excellence
   - Signal: AI usage exists but isn't systematic or scalable

4. **Individual Career Development:**
   - Unclear how to progress beyond basic AI usage
   - Want systematic approach to skill building
   - Need to demonstrate AI competency for new role
   - Signal: Using AI daily but feeling "stuck" at current level

5. **Competitive Positioning:**
   - Competitors adopting AI systematically
   - Industry baseline shifting rapidly
   - Need sustainable competitive advantage in AI
   - Signal: "Everyone has AI now, how do we differentiate?"

6. **Technology Transition Periods:**
   - New AI capabilities launch (agents, voice, etc.)
   - Need to evaluate relevance to organization
   - Want to map new tech to existing competencies
   - Signal: "Should we adopt [new AI feature]?" without clear evaluation criteria

### When NOT to Use This Pattern

**Avoid this framework when:**

1. **Immediate, Specific Skill Needed:**
   - Need someone to execute specific task today
   - Hiring for specialized AI engineering role
   - Time-sensitive project requires specific tool mastery
   - Alternative: Use tool-specific training for immediate need, then build foundation

2. **Technical AI Development Roles:**
   - Building AI systems (not just using them)
   - Need understanding of model architecture, training, deployment
   - Role requires coding AI applications
   - Alternative: This framework is user-focused; use technical competency frameworks instead

3. **Very Early Exploration:**
   - Organization has zero AI exposure
   - Need to build basic comfort before systematic approach
   - Cultural resistance to AI adoption
   - Alternative: Start with safe experimentation, then introduce framework

4. **Compliance-Driven Environments:**
   - Highly regulated industry with strict AI usage policies
   - Need audit trails and approval processes
   - Innovation/discovery could create compliance risk
   - Alternative: Use framework but cap progression at systematic levels (5-7)

5. **Resource-Constrained Situations:**
   - No time for 90-day development plans
   - Can't invest in systematic skill building
   - Need immediate productivity gains only
   - Alternative: Focus on high-yield prompt templates for quick wins

6. **When Tool Lock-In Required:**
   - Committed to single vendor ecosystem
   - Integrations require tool-specific expertise
   - Model-agnostic approach creates confusion
   - Alternative: Layer this framework underneath tool-specific training

### General Principles

1. **Principle: Horizontal Before Vertical**
   - Build model-agnostic fluency foundation first
   - Then apply to specific business context
   - Enables adaptation as tools evolve

2. **Principle: Systems Create Scale**

   - Document patterns, don't just execute them
   - Application: Identify one "AI champion" per function to systematize workflows

3. **Principle: Teaching Accelerates Learning**

   - Lunch & learns, documentation requirements for senior staff
   - Application: Make teaching part of advancement criteria to Level 7+

4. **Principle: Prompt Yield as KPI**
   - Measure and optimize for quality per interaction
   - Better than "AI usage hours" or "number of prompts"
   - Application: Track prompt yield by function, identify systematization opportunities

5. **Principle: Baseline is Moving**
   - Today's Level 3 is tomorrow's Level 1
   - Continuous development required to maintain competitive position
   - Application: Quarterly fluency reassessments, updated 90-day plans

## Strategic Patterns Identified

### Pattern 1: Capability Ladder Design
**Pattern:** Create progressive skill framework where each level builds on previous while adding distinct capabilities. Make assessment behavioral (what people do) rather than credential-based (what courses they took). Intentionally make it challenging ("tough scale") to create accurate differentiation and prevent grade inflation.

**Why It Works:** Behavioral indicators are harder to game than credentials. Progressive structure shows clear path forward while acknowledging current position. Difficulty creates signal value - everyone claiming Level 9 would make framework worthless.

**Application:** Any domain with rapidly evolving tool landscape but stable underlying principles. Could apply to: data analysis fluency, digital marketing sophistication, remote work effectiveness.

### Pattern 2: Abstraction Layer for Durability
**Pattern:** Build framework one abstraction level above specific implementations (tools) to survive churn. Focus on mental models and principles that persist across versions/vendors. Make it explicitly "model-agnostic" and "evergreen."

**Why It Works:** Tools change faster than principles. "How LLMs work" remains relevant across ChatGPT 4, 5, 6, etc. Investment in learning framework compounds rather than becoming obsolete. Creates "living breathing framework that helps you make sense of your own skill level relative to where we are with AI that you can come back to again and again."

**Application:** Any fast-moving technology domain. Could apply to: social media marketing (platforms change, audience psychology doesn't), programming languages (syntax changes, algorithms don't), software tools in general.

### Pattern 3: Teaching as Forcing Function
**Pattern:** Structure highest mastery levels around teaching/documentation requirements. Make knowledge sharing integral to advancement, not auxiliary. Use teaching to reveal gaps in understanding and accelerate learning.

**Why It Works:** "Teaching has been super helpful in driving clarity and revealing gaps in my own understanding that I have to relentlessly close. Most teachers will tell you regardless of subject that that's true." Creates virtuous cycle: teaching reveals gaps → close gaps → better teaching → more gap discovery. Also scales organizational capability through documentation.

**Application:** Any expertise domain where knowledge needs to scale. Could apply to: technical skills in growing startups, craft/trade knowledge, professional services expertise. Make "taught X people" a promotion requirement, not optional mentorship.

# Everyone's Chasing AI Skills—But Judgement is Now Priceless

## Summary
As AI-generated intelligence becomes "too cheap to meter" (dropping 40x per year according to Sam Altman), value is migrating from analytical capability to human judgment. Nate Jones introduces the concept of "judgment merchants"—people who can identify bottlenecks, understand context deeply, sequence decisions effectively, and encode judgment into lasting systems. This 10-principle framework for cultivating judgment applies equally to internal employees and external consultants, as traditional role boundaries blur in the AI age. The core thesis: AI excels at analysis but fails at the judgment required to turn intelligence into action.

---

## 1. Context

**Background:** The video addresses the fundamental shift happening as AI makes intelligence abundant and cheap. While everyone is racing to acquire AI skills, a new bottleneck has emerged: the ability to exercise good business judgment. The speaker coins the term "judgment merchants" to describe professionals who can navigate this new landscape, whether working as external consultants or internal employees.

**Why This Matters:** This represents a fundamental revaluation of professional capabilities. Organizations that previously valued analytical horsepower must now identify and develop judgment capabilities across all levels and job families. This shift challenges traditional consulting models, internal promotion criteria, and skill development frameworks. For 1658 Holdings, this suggests a critical competitive advantage in developing judgment capabilities systematically rather than treating it as an innate trait only found at senior levels.

**Key Stats:** 
- Intelligence cost falling 40x per year (Sam Altman's estimate)
- Intelligence becoming "too cheap to meter"
- Traditional consulting busy work (deck production) becoming automated
- Judgment historically reserved for principal-level roles now needed across all levels

---

## 2. Vision & Why

**Core Mission:** To systematically develop judgment as a trainable, identifiable capability rather than an abstract quality that emerges mysteriously at senior levels. The goal is to make everyone in an organization effective "judgment merchants" who can turn abundant AI-generated intelligence into valuable action.

**The "Why" Behind It:** For decades, exercising good business judgment was "the bar for principal product manager, the bar for a senior engineering leader." This capability was learned through osmosis from other senior practitioners. Now that AI has democratized intelligence production, judgment cannot remain an elite skill—it must be taught systematically to "every level, for every job family" because "intelligence is bottlenecked on good judgment."

**Enduring Nature:** 
- **Timeless:** The core principles of judgment (understanding constraints, sequencing decisions, building coalitions, taking ownership) remain constant
- **2024-2026 Specific:** The urgency and universality of judgment development; the specific ways AI creates new bottlenecks; the blurring of consultant/employee boundaries; the need for transparent reasoning to distinguish human thought from AI output

---

## 3. Strategic Engine

**How This Actually Works:** The strategic engine operates on the principle that value migrates to scarcity. As AI makes intelligence abundant, the bottleneck shifts to judgment—the ability to find what's scarce, understand context, sequence actions, build coalitions, and encode decisions into systems. Organizations develop competitive advantage by systematically cultivating these judgment capabilities rather than treating them as innate talents.

**Key Components:**
1. **Scarcity Detection:** Finding bottlenecks in systems where intelligence has unlocked volume
2. **Context Discrimination:** Combining pattern recognition with awareness of what makes each situation unique
3. **Constraint Mapping:** Understanding what's possible within current organizational reality
4. **Sequential Thinking:** Ordering bets to create momentum and proof before resistance mounts
5. **Coalition Architecture:** Mapping stakeholder relationships and planning conviction moments

**Why This Works:** This approach works because it addresses the actual failure modes of AI-augmented work. AI can generate endless analysis and options (paralysis through abundance), but it fails at prioritization, context-specific application, stakeholder navigation, and taking ownership of outcomes. By systematically developing these complementary capabilities, organizations create sustainable competitive advantage in an age of commoditized intelligence.

---

## 4. Behavioral Design

**Behavioral Principles:**
1. **Accountability Over Output:** "You are accountable for every word you write. It can be with AI, but you're still accountable."
2. **Transparent Reasoning:** In an age of AI-generated polish, showing your thinking process signals genuine judgment
3. **Explicit Deprioritization:** Regularly identifying and defending what you will NOT do
4. **Feedback Loops:** Actively seeking calibration on what works and doesn't work
5. **Conviction Sequencing:** Planning moments that shift stakeholders from permission to ownership

**Incentive Structure:**
- **Encourages:** Finding bottlenecks, understanding context deeply, saying no, transparent trade-off analysis, owning consequences
- **Discourages:** Shipping first-draft AI output, scope expansion, opacity, over-analysis without decision, personal heroics over system building

**Alignment Mechanisms:**
- Non-goals lists (explicit statements of what won't be pursued)
- Conviction moment planning (structured stakeholder engagement)
- Calibration cycles (rapid feedback on judgment quality)
- Ownership statements ("If I'm wrong, here's how we'll know it, and here's what we'll do")

---

## 5. Time & Attention

**Where Time Flows:**
- **Finding Bottlenecks:** Identifying where AI-unlocked volume creates new constraints (customer attention, implementation capacity, human resources, selection)
- **Context Development:** Deep understanding of what makes this situation, organization, or moment unique
- **Coalition Building:** Mapping decision-maker relationships and sequencing buy-in
- **Calibration:** Getting feedback on judgment accuracy to compound capability over time
- **System Encoding:** Translating judgment into playbooks and automations others can run

**What This System DOESN'T Spend On:**
- Generating initial analysis (AI handles this)
- Producing polished presentations (AI commoditizes this)
- Expanding scope and options (AI naturally does this)
- Personal heroics that don't transfer (focus is on organizational capability)
- Hiding reasoning or trade-offs (transparency is the new value signal)

**Allocation Philosophy:** Time flows toward activities that AI cannot replicate well: finding what's scarce, understanding unique context, sequencing for political/organizational reality, taking ownership, and encoding judgment into lasting systems. The philosophy recognizes that "analysis by itself is paralyzing" while judgment creates actionable direction.

---

## 6. Moats & Time Horizon

**Competitive Advantages:**
1. **Context Moat:** Deep understanding of specific organizational context is non-transferable and hard for competitors to replicate
2. **Coalition Knowledge:** Mapping of decision-maker relationships and conviction sequencing specific to your organization
3. **Calibration Advantage:** Judgment compounds through feedback loops—early starters build better judgment faster
4. **System Encoding:** Organizations that capture judgment in playbooks create compounding advantages
5. **Trust Premium:** In an age of AI slop, demonstrated judgment builds irreplaceable trust

**Time Horizon:**
- **Short-term (0-6 months):** Demonstrate judgment through accurate bottleneck identification, explicit non-goals, and transparent reasoning
- **Medium-term (6-24 months):** Build calibration advantage through rapid feedback cycles on judgment accuracy
- **Long-term (2+ years):** Create organizational capability through encoded judgment in systems, developing judgment culture across all levels

**Why Time Is Your Friend:** Judgment compounds through calibration—"your judgment gets better as you get feedback on what goes right and what does not go right." Organizations that start systematically developing judgment create increasing gaps versus competitors still treating it as an innate senior-level trait. The competitive advantage grows as encoded judgment creates systems that multiply individual judgment capabilities.

---

## 7. Flywheels & Lock-In

**Primary Flywheel:** The Judgment Calibration Flywheel

**Flywheel Visualization:**
[Exercise Judgment on Projects] → [Get Rapid Feedback on What Worked/Failed] → [Calibrate Understanding of Context, Constraints, and Coalitions] → [Apply Improved Judgment to Next Projects] → [Faster, More Accurate Decisions] → [Greater Trust and Scope] → [More Opportunities to Exercise Judgment] → [Back to Step 1, with better calibration]

**Lock-In Mechanisms:**
1. **Knowledge Lock-In:** Deep context understanding becomes increasingly valuable and non-transferable
2. **Relationship Lock-In:** Coalition maps and stakeholder conviction sequences are specific to each organization
3. **Calibration Lock-In:** Judgment improves through organizational-specific feedback loops
4. **System Lock-In:** Encoded judgment in playbooks and automations creates organizational dependency
5. **Trust Lock-In:** Demonstrated judgment builds trust that's difficult for outsiders to replicate quickly

**Compounding Effect:** 
- Individual judgment improves fractally: career-level (years), project-level (months), decision-level (days)
- AI projects provide especially rapid feedback cycles for calibration
- Encoded judgment in systems multiplies individual capability across the organization
- "Judgment shifts from personal heroics to true organizational capability"
- Each judgment exercise creates data for better next judgments

---

## 8. System Beneficiaries

**Winners:**
1. **Judgment-Capable Professionals:** Those who develop systematic judgment skills become irreplaceable as AI commoditizes analysis
2. **Organizations with Judgment Culture:** Companies that develop judgment across all levels gain massive competitive advantage
3. **Context Specialists:** Professionals who get "passionate about a particular corner of context" become uniquely valuable
4. **Consultants Who Offer True Judgment:** External advisors who provide genuine judgment (vs. polished decks) become "priceless"
5. **Early Adopters:** Individuals and organizations that start calibration loops early compound advantages

**Losers:**
1. **Analysis-Only Professionals:** Those who only generate intelligence without judgment become commoditized
2. **Traditional Consultants:** Those relying on "busy work in producing decks" see their value proposition evaporate
3. **Opacity-Reliant Advisors:** Those whose value came from opaque expertise lose differentiation
4. **Senior-Only Judgment Models:** Organizations that reserve judgment for principals miss the democratization opportunity
5. **AI-Dependent Decision Makers:** Those who ship "first draft out of Chat GPT" without judgment create organizational damage

**Ethical Considerations:**
- Risk of judgment becoming gatekeeping mechanism if not democratized
- Danger of premature optimization (declaring non-goals too early without exploration)
- Potential for judgment frameworks to become rigid rules rather than flexible principles
- Accountability requirements may create risk-aversion if not balanced with learning culture
- Coalition-building could become manipulation if not grounded in genuine value creation

---

## 9. System Health Metric

**What to Optimize For:** **Judgment Calibration Velocity** - The speed at which individuals and organizations improve judgment accuracy through feedback loops on decisions, measured by the cycle time from decision to feedback to improved next decision.

**Why This Metric:** This metric captures the essence of judgment as a compounding capability. Unlike static measures (like "percentage of good decisions"), calibration velocity measures the learning rate. It recognizes that judgment isn't about being right all the time but about getting better faster than alternatives. Organizations with high calibration velocity will outperform those with initially better judgment but slower learning. This metric also naturally encourages the behaviors that matter: making explicit predictions, seeking feedback, updating mental models, and applying learning to next decisions.

**How to Measure:**
1. **Track Decision Cycle Time:** Time from decision → outcome visibility → feedback integration → improved next decision
2. **Measure Prediction Accuracy Improvement:** Rate of improvement in accuracy of "if/then" statements over time
3. **Monitor Calibration Events:** Frequency of explicit "here's what I expected vs. what happened" reviews
4. **Assess Transfer Speed:** How quickly judgment improvements in one domain apply to adjacent domains
5. **Count System Encodings:** Frequency of judgment being captured in playbooks/automations that others use

**Practical Tracking:**
- Monthly judgment reviews: "What did we predict? What actually happened? What changed in our mental model?"
- Project post-mortems focused on judgment quality, not just outcomes
- Ratio of projects with explicit non-goals to total projects
- Stakeholder feedback on judgment quality (not just outcomes)
- Number of judgment principles encoded into organizational systems

---

## 10. Unique Insights & Quotes

### Memorable Quotes

> "We're all becoming judgment merchants. I know that's a new word. I'm coining it."

> "Intelligence is becoming too cheap to meter. Sam Alman was saying that intelligence is falling 40x a year in cost."

> "Judgment is not a taught skill. This is not something that you can just go and say, 'Oh, I know judgment now. I'm good at judgment.'"

> "Value migrates to the next bottleneck. Basically, one of the ways you show value, whether you are inside a company... everywhere you look on an AI project, you will see places where intelligence unlocks an enormous amount of volume and you will see places where that volume bottlenecks."

> "Good judgment is excellent pattern recognition crossed with excellent context discrimination."

> "AI loves to expand scope. One of the signals of good judgment in 2025 of someone who is accountable for their work is they will be honest with you at a moment's notice about what they are saying no to, what they are deprioritizing, what they cannot do."

> "The fastest signal of good judgment is how precisely you can define the true current bottleneck."

> "Most insights fail because timing is bad or sequencing is bad."

> "The fastest way to fix AI slop at work is to tell everyone, you are accountable for every word you write. It can be with AI, but you're still accountable."

> "Judgment is becoming more valuable. Judgment is the new bottleneck. Judgment is what is becoming scarce when analysis is free."

### Non-Obvious Insights

- **Transparency as New Value Signal:** In the old model, polished opacity signaled value; now transparent reasoning signals genuine judgment because AI has commoditized polish. The shift means showing your thinking process is more valuable than hiding it.

- **Consultants and Employees Converging:** The traditional boundary between internal employees and external consultants is blurring because both now compete on judgment rather than specialized analysis. "We are all becoming consultants" as judgment becomes universal currency.

- **Deprioritization as Competitive Advantage:** Explicitly listing non-goals is a differentiator because AI naturally expands scope. The discipline of saying "not this, not this, not this" is something AI is "terrible at" and humans must master.

- **Conviction Moments vs. Permission:** Good judgment includes "planning conviction moments where stakeholders experience early wins and shift their stance from merely permitting something to happen to active ownership." It's not about getting approval but engineering belief.

- **Fractal Calibration:** Judgment improvement operates at multiple timescales simultaneously—career level (years), project level (months), decision level (days)—with AI projects providing especially fast feedback loops for rapid improvement.

- **System Encoding as Final Leverage:** Individual judgment only reaches full value when "encoded into systems that last," creating "true organizational capability" rather than relying on "personal heroics." This is the ultimate force multiplier.

- **Context as Moat:** "Get passionate about a particular corner of context" because deep context understanding is inherently non-transferable and provides sustainable competitive advantage in an age of abundant general intelligence.

- **Scarcity Principle Application:** The bottleneck isn't always obvious—it might be "scarcity of selection," "scarcity of sequencing," "scarcity of implementation," "scarcity of human resources," or even "customer attention is scarce." Finding the true constraint is the first judgment call.

- **Thin Slicing for Trust:** "Thin slicing value" isn't about minimal viable product—it's about sequencing bets to deliver something quickly that "earns trust" and creates momentum before resistance builds. The judgment is in the sequencing, not the sizing.

- **Accountability as Antidote:** The clearest tell of good judgment isn't accuracy but "willingness to say, 'If I'm wrong, here's how we'll know it, and here's what we'll do.'" Ownership distinguishes judgment from analysis.

---

## 11. Application & Mental Model

### When to Use This Pattern

**Applicable Conditions:**
- When AI/automation has created abundance in one area (data, analysis, options, intelligence) but bottlenecks emerge elsewhere
- When traditional expertise is being commoditized and you need to identify new sources of value
- When decision-making speed matters more than perfect information
- When stakeholder alignment is as important as technical correctness
- When building organizational capability matters more than individual heroics
- When feedback cycles are fast enough to enable calibration
- When transparency can be a competitive advantage rather than vulnerability

**Signal Indicators:**
- Teams are drowning in analysis but struggling to decide
- AI tools are producing lots of content but quality/judgment is inconsistent
- Senior leadership is bottlenecked on too many decisions
- Projects fail despite good analysis (timing, sequencing, coalition problems)
- Consultants are producing deliverables but not driving change
- Role boundaries are becoming unclear (who decides what?)

### When NOT to Use This Pattern

**Backfire Conditions:**
- When situations are truly novel with no pattern recognition possible (pure exploration phase)
- When technical expertise genuinely matters more than judgment (specialized engineering, compliance, etc.)
- When feedback cycles are too slow for calibration (decisions with multi-year outcome visibility)
- When organizational culture punishes being wrong (judgment requires safe experimentation)
- When stakeholder relationships are too damaged for coalition building to work
- When speed of decision matters less than correctness (safety-critical, irreversible decisions)
- When opacity is genuinely required (competitive strategy, M&A, sensitive HR matters)

**Inappropriate Situations:**
- Early-stage research where exploration should be unconstrained
- Crisis situations requiring immediate technical expertise over judgment development
- Highly regulated environments where process compliance trumps judgment
- When organizational capability doesn't matter (one-off decisions, external hires)
- When judgment frameworks could calcify into bureaucratic rules

### How to Apply to 1658 Holdings Companies

**Finland DMC Oy:**
- **Context Specialization:** Develop deep judgment on Finland-specific travel patterns, seasonal demand bottlenecks, and supplier relationship dynamics that AI can't replicate
- **Expected Outcome:** Become irreplaceable to tour operators by offering judgment on which experiences will work for which client segments in which seasons, not just information about Finland
- **Coalition Mapping:** Map relationships with key suppliers, tourism boards, and corporate clients to sequence conviction moments for new service offerings
- **Non-Goals:** Explicitly define what tourism experiences you won't offer (even if AI suggests them) to maintain focus and quality
- **System Encoding:** Capture judgment about seasonal planning, supplier reliability, and client preferences in playbooks that enable rapid onboarding of new team members

**General Principles for 1658 Holdings:**

1. **Develop Judgment Systematically Across All Portfolio Companies**
   - Create shared frameworks for judgment development (adapted versions of these 10 principles)
   - Establish cross-company calibration sessions where portfolio companies share "what we predicted vs. what happened"
   - Build judgment as a shared capability rather than treating it as CEO-only responsibility
   - Measure judgment calibration velocity as a portfolio-wide metric

2. **Use AI to Unlock Volume, Then Apply Judgment to Bottlenecks**
   - Deploy AI for market research, competitive analysis, content generation, data analysis
   - Train teams to identify where AI-unlocked volume creates new bottlenecks (implementation capacity, customer attention, supplier relationships)
   - Value creation shifts from producing intelligence to finding and addressing bottlenecks
   - Example: AI generates 100 content ideas → judgment determines which 3 to pursue and in what sequence

3. **Build Coalition Architecture for Portfolio Decisions**
   - Map stakeholder relationships across portfolio companies (who influences whom?)
   - Sequence conviction moments for major portfolio decisions (not just seeking approval)
   - Plan thin-sliced value delivery to build momentum before resistance mounts
   - Create explicit "if wrong, here's what we do" statements for major bets to build trust

4. **Create Judgment Encoding Systems**
   - Capture judgment in playbooks, not just in individual leaders' heads
   - Build systems that let others apply proven judgment without starting from scratch
   - Measure success by organizational capability, not individual heroics
   - Enable faster scaling of new portfolio companies by transferring encoded judgment

5. **Establish Transparent Reasoning as Cultural Norm**
   - Make explicit trade-off analysis a requirement for major proposals
   - Require non-goals lists for all significant initiatives
   - Value transparency about assumptions and reasoning over polished presentations
   - Build trust through demonstrated judgment, not through opacity

---

## Strategic Patterns Identified

1. **Value Migration to Scarcity:** When technology makes something abundant (intelligence/analysis), value systematically migrates to whatever becomes scarce (judgment/decision-making). This pattern repeats across technological transitions—identify what's becoming abundant and invest in the complementary scarce resource.

2. **Human-AI Complementarity as Competitive Advantage:** The winning strategy isn't human vs. AI or human replaced by AI—it's systematically developing the human capabilities that AI amplifies poorly (judgment, context, coalition-building, ownership). Organizations that identify and cultivate these complementary capabilities create sustainable moats.

3. **Democratization of Elite Skills:** When AI lowers barriers to advanced capabilities, previously elite skills must be democratized across organizational levels to maintain competitive advantage. Judgment was historically a principal-level trait; now it must be "every level, for every job family" or the organization becomes bottlenecked on a few senior decision-makers.
